### TURKEY
Orders: `A BUL H` · `F BLA S A BUL` · `F CON H` · `A ANK - ARM`

<details><summary>Reasoning</summary>

> Looking at my position: I have 4 units (F BLA, A BUL, A ANK, F CON) and face coordinated attacks from Austria (A SER + A BUD → BUL), Italy (F AEG → CON, F ARM → ANK), and Russia (F RUM → BUL/EC).
>
> My revised strategy says: defend BUL at maximum strength with F BLA supporting A BUL's hold, move F CON to support A BUL as well, and move A ANK to ARM to pressure Italy's F ARM.
>
> The key question is whether F CON should support A BUL or do something else. If Italy's F AEG moves to CON, and F CON is supporting BUL, then CON falls. But if I move F CON away to defend itself, BUL falls to Austria's 2-unit attack.
>
> With F BLA S A BUL and F CON S A BUL, BUL has strength 3 against Austria's 2-unit attack (A SER + A BUD support = strength 2). BUL holds! But CON falls to Italy's F AEG (strength 1 vs empty CON).
>
> If I instead use F CON H to defend CON, Italy's F AEG bounces CON (1 vs 1), but then BUL has only F BLA supporting = strength 2 vs Austria's strength 2 = bounce. BUL holds too, and CON holds!
>
> Wait - F CON H means CON defends at strength 1, and Italy's F AEG attacks at strength 1 = bounce. BUL has F BLA support = strength 2 vs Austria's strength 2 = bounce. Both hold!
>
> But Russia's F RUM → BUL/EC is also attacking BUL. With F BLA supporting BUL, BUL defends at strength 2. Austria attacks at 2 (A SER + A BUD support). Russia attacks at 1. Two attackers can't both take BUL simultaneously - they'd need to both succeed, but with equal strength to the defender, BUL bounces both.
>
> Actually: multiple attackers of different origins don't combine. Each attack resolves separately. BUL at strength 2 (F BLA support) holds against Austria at 2 (bounce) and Russia at 1 (repelled). CON at strength 1 (holding) bounces Italy at 1.
>
> So: F CON H, F BLA S A BUL, A BUL H, A ANK - ARM seems best - holds both BUL and CON!

</details>
